**Tamil Nadu**[^cite_note-7] is the southernmost [state](375986.md) of India. The [tenth largest Indian state by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_area) and the [sixth largest by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_population), it is the home of the [Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamils), who speak the [Tamil language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language), the state's official language and the first to be recognised as a [classical language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_languages_of_India) in India. [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai) is the state's largest city and capital.

Located on the south-eastern coast of the [Indian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_peninsula), Tamil Nadu is straddled by the [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats) and [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) in the west, the [Eastern Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ghats) in the north, the [Eastern Coastal Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Coastal_Plains) lining the [Bay of Bengal](47892.md) in the east, the [Gulf of Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar) and the [Palk Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palk_Strait) to the south-east, the [Laccadive Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laccadive_Sea) at the southern [cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_\(geography\)) of the peninsula, with the river [Kaveri](177299.md) bisecting the state. Politically, Tamil Nadu is bound by the Indian states of [Kerala](4349459.md), [Karnataka](16880.md), and [Andhra Pradesh](2377.md), and encloses a part of the [union territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_territory) of [Puducherry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puducherry_\(union_territory\)). It shares an international [maritime border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_border) with the [Northern Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Province,_Sri_Lanka) of [Sri Lanka](26750.md) at [Pamban Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamban_Island).

[Archaeological evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_evidence) indicates that the Tamil Nadu region could have been inhabited more than 385,000 years ago by [archaic humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaic_humans). The state has more than 5,500 years of continuous cultural history. [Historically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Tamil_Nadu), the [Tamilakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamilakam) region was inhabited by Tamil-speaking [Dravidian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_people), who were ruled by several regimes over centuries such as the [Sangam era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_era) [triumvirate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Crowned_Kings) of the [Cheras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheras), [Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholas) and [Pandyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandyas), the [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallavas) (3rd–9th century CE), and the later [Vijayanagara Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_Empire) (14th–17th century CE). [European colonisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_India) began with establishing trade ports in the 17th century, with the [British controlling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj) much of the state as a part of the [Madras Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Presidency) for two centuries. After the [Indian Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India's_Independence) in 1947, the region became the [Madras State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_State) of the Republic of India and was further reorganised when [states were redrawn linguistically in 1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Act,_1956) into its current shape. The state was renamed as Tamil Nadu, meaning "Tamil Country", in 1969. Hence, [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_culture), [cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_cuisine) and [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Tamil_Nadu) have seen multiple influences over the years and have developed diversely.

As of December 2023, Tamil Nadu had an [economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Tamil_Nadu) with a gross state domestic product (GSDP) of [₹](490783.md)27.22 trillion (US\$280 billion), making it the [second-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP) economy amongst the 28 states of India. It has the country's [9th-highest GSDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP_per_capita) of [₹](490783.md)315,220 (US\$3,300) and ranks [11th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_territories_by_Human_Development_Index) in [human development index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_development_index). Tamil Nadu is also one of the most industrialised states, with the [manufacturing sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_sector) accounting for nearly one-third of the state's GDP. With its diverse culture and architecture, long coastline, forests and mountains, Tamil Nadu is home to a number of ancient relics, historic buildings, religious sites, [beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaches), [hill stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_stations), [forts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forts), [waterfalls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterfalls) and four [World Heritage Sites](44940.md). The state's [tourism industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Tamil_Nadu) is the largest among the Indian states. The state has three [biosphere reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biosphere_reserves), [mangrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) forests, five [National Parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Parks), 18 [wildlife sanctuaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_sanctuaries) and 17 [bird sanctuaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird_sanctuaries). The [Tamil film industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_film_industry), nicknamed as Kollywood, plays an influential role in the state's popular culture.

## Etymology

The name is derived from [Tamil language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) with *nadu* meaning "land" and Tamil Nadu meaning "the land of Tamils". The [origin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language#Etymology) and precise etymology of the word Tamil is unclear with multiple theories attested to it.[^cite_note-Zvelebil-8] In the ancient [Sangam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_literature), [Tamilakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamilakam) refers to the area of present-day Tamil Nadu, [Kerala](4349459.md) and parts of [Karnataka](16880.md) and [Andhra Pradesh](2377.md). *[Tolkāppiyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tolk%C4%81ppiyam)* (2nd to 1st century BCE) indicates the borders of Tamilakam as [Tirumala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirumala) and [Kanniyakumari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanniyakumari).[^cite_note-9] The name *Tamilakam* is used in other Sangam era literature such as *[Puṟanāṉūṟu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pu%E1%B9%9Fan%C4%81%E1%B9%89%C5%AB%E1%B9%9Fu)*, *[Patiṟṟuppattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pati%E1%B9%9F%E1%B9%9Fuppattu)*, *[Cilappatikaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cilappatikaram)*, and *[Manimekalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manimekalai)*.[^cite_note-10] *Cilappatikaram* (5th to 6th century CE) and *[Ramavataram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramavataram)* (12th century CE) mention the name *Tamil Nadu* to denote the region.[^cite_note-11][^cite_note-12][^cite_note-13]

## History

Main article: [History of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Tamil_Nadu)

### Prehistory (before 5th century BCE)

[Archaeological evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_evidence) indicates that the Tamil Nadu region could have been inhabited more than 385,000 years ago by [archaic humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaic_humans).[^cite_note-14][^cite_note-15] Artifacts recovered in [Adichanallur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adichanallur) by the [Archaeological Survey of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Survey_of_India) (ASI) indicate a continuous history from more than 3,800 years ago.[^cite_note-16] [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) [celts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celt_\(tool\)) with the [Indus script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_script) dated between 1500 and 2000 BCE indicate the use of the [Harappan language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harappan_language).[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] Excavations at [Keezhadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keezhadi_excavation_site) have revealed a large urban settlement, with the earliest artefact dated to 580 BCE, during the time of urbanisation in the [Indo-Gangetic plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Gangetic_plain).[^cite_note-19] Further epigraphical inscriptions found at Adichanallur use [Tamil Brahmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Brahmi), a rudimentary script dated to 5th century BCE.[^cite_note-20] Potsherds uncovered from Keeladi indicate a script which might be a transition between the Indus Valley script and Tamil Brahmi script used later.[^cite_note-21]

### Sangam period (5th century BCE – 3rd century CE)

Main articles: [Sangam period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_period), [Tamilakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamilakam), and [Sangam landscape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_landscape)



[Tamilakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamilakam) during the [Sangam Period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_Period) (500 BCE–300 CE)

The [Sangam period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_period) lasted for about eight centuries, from 500 BCE to 300 CE with the main source of history during the period coming from the Sangam literature.[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] Ancient [Tamilakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamilakam) was ruled by a [triumvirate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Crowned_Kings) of monarchical states, [Cheras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheras), [Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholas) and [Pandyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandyas).[^cite_note-24] The Cheras controlled the western part of Tamilkam, the Pandyas controlled the south, and the Cholas had their base in the [Kaveri](177299.md) delta. The kings called *Vendhar* ruled over several tribes of *Velala* (peasants), headed by the *[Velir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velir)* chiefs.[^cite_note-25] The rulers patronised multiple religions including [vedic religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_Vedic_religion), [Buddhism](3267529.md) and [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism) and sponsored some of the earliest Tamil literature with the oldest surviving work being [Tolkāppiyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tolk%C4%81ppiyam), a book of Tamil grammar.[^cite_note-26]

The kingdoms had significant diplomatic and trade contacts with other kingdoms to the north and with the [Romans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire).[^cite_note-27] Much of the commerce from the Romans and Han China were facilitated via seaports including [Muziris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muziris) and [Korkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korkai) with [spices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spices) being the most prized goods along with [pearls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearls) and [silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk).[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29] From 300 CE, the region was ruled by the [Kalabhras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalabhras), warriors belonging to the [Vellalar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vellalar) community, who were once feudatories of the three ancient Tamil kingdoms.[^cite_note-30] The Kalabhra era is referred to as the "dark period" of Tamil history, and information about it is generally inferred from any mentions in the literature and inscriptions that are dated many centuries after their era ended.[^cite_note-31] The twin Tamil epics [Cilappatikaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cilappatikaram) and [Manimekalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manimekalai) were written during the era.[^cite_note-32] Tamil classic [Tirukkural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirukkural) by [Thiruvalluvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvalluvar), a collection of couplets is attributed to the same period.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34]

### Medieval era (4th–13th century CE)



[Rock cut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_cut) monuments in [Mahabalipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabalipuram) built by the [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallavas)

Around the 7th century CE, the Kalabhras were overthrown by the Pandyas and Cholas, who patronised Buddhism and Jainism before the revival of [Saivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saivism) and [Vaishnavism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaishnavism) during the [Bhakti movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhakti_movement).[^cite_note-35] Though they existed previously, the period saw the rise of the [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallavas) in the sixth century CE under [Mahendravarman I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahendravarman_I), who ruled parts of [South India](678583.md) with [Kanchipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram) as their capital.[^cite_note-36] The Pallavas were noted for their patronage of architecture: the massive [gopuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram), ornate towers at the entrance of temples, originated with the [Pallava architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava_architecture). They built the group of [rock-cut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_cut) monuments in [Mahabalipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabalipuram) and temples in Kanchipuram.[^cite_note-UNC-37] Throughout their reign, the Pallavas remained in constant conflict with the Cholas and Pandyas. The Pandyas were revived by [Kadungon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadungon) towards the end of the 6th century CE and with the Cholas in obscurity in [Uraiyur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uraiyur), the Tamil country was divided between the Pallavas and the Pandyas.[^cite_note-38] The [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aparajitavarman) were finally defeated by Chola prince [Aditya I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aditya_I) in the 9th century CE.[^cite_note-39]



The [Chola Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_Empire) at its greatest extent, during the reign of [Rajendra Chola I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajendra_Chola_I) in 1030

The Cholas became the dominant kingdom in the 9th century under [Vijayalaya Chola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayalaya_Chola), who established [Thanjavur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur) as Chola's new capital with further expansions by subsequent rulers. In the 11th century CE, [Rajaraja I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajaraja_I) expanded the Chola Empire with conquests of entire Southern India and parts of present-day [Sri Lanka](26750.md) and [Maldives](19117.md), and increased Chola influence across the [Indian Ocean](14580.md).[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-41] Rajaraja brought in administrative reforms including the reorganisation of Tamil country into individual administrative units.[^cite_note-42] Under his son [Rajendra Chola I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajendra_Chola_I), the Chola Empire reached its zenith and stretched as far as [Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal) in the north and across the Indian Ocean.[^cite_note-43] The Cholas built many temples in the [Dravidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_architecture) style with the most notable being the [Brihadisvara Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihadisvara_Temple) at Thanjavur, one of the foremost temples of the era built by Rajaraja, and [Gangaikonda Cholapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangaikonda_Cholapuram), built by Rajendra.[^cite_note-Great_Living_Chola_Temples-44]

The Pandyas again reigned supreme early in the 13th century under [Maravarman Sundara I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maravarman_Sundara_Pandyan).[^cite_note-45] They ruled from their capital of [Madurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurai) and expanded trade links with other maritime empires.[^cite_note-46] During the 13th century, [Marco Polo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Polo) mentioned the Pandyas as the richest empire in existence. The Pandyas also built a number of temples including the [Meenakshi Amman Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meenakshi_Amman_Temple) at Madurai.[^cite_note-47]

### Vijayanagar and Nayak period (14th–17th century CE)

In the 13th and 14th centuries, there were repeated attacks from [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate).[^cite_note-48] The [Vijayanagara kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_empire) was founded in 1336 CE.[^cite_note-49] The Vijayanagara empire eventually conquered the entire Tamil country by c. 1370 and ruled for almost two centuries until its defeat in the [Battle of Talikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Talikota) in 1565 by a confederacy of [Deccan sultanates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_sultanates).[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] Later, the [Nayaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayak_dynasty), who were the military governors in the Vijaynagara Empire, took control of the region amongst whom the [Nayaks of Madurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayaks_of_Madurai) and [Nayaks of Thanjavur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayaks_of_Thanjavur) were the most prominent.[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53] They introduced the [palayakkararar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygar) system and re-constructed some of the well-known temples in Tamil Nadu including the Meenakshi Temple in Madurai.[^cite_note-54]

### Later conflicts and European colonisation (17th to 20th century CE)

In the 18th century, the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire) administered the region through the [Nawab of the Carnatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawab_of_the_Carnatic) with his seat at [Arcot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcot), who defeated the Madurai Nayaks.[^cite_note-55] The [Marathas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathas) [attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Trichinopoly_\(1741\)) several times and defeated the Nawab after the [Siege of Trichinopoly (1751–1752)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Trichinopoly_\(1751%E2%80%931752\)).[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58] This led to a short-lived [Thanjavur Maratha kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur_Maratha_kingdom).[^cite_note-59]



[Fort Dansborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Dansborg) at [Tharangambadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tharangambadi), built by the [Danes](419786.md)

Europeans started to establish trade centres from the 16th century along the eastern coast. The [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Portugal) arrived in 1522 and built a port named [São Tomé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_de_Meliapore) near present-day [Mylapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mylapore) in Madras.[^cite_note-60] In 1609, the [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_people) established a settlement in [Pulicat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulicat) and the [Danes](419786.md) had their establishment in [Tharangambadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tharangambadi).[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] On 20 August 1639, Francis Day of the [British East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_East_India_Company) met with the Vijayanager emperor [Peda Venkata Raya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peda_Venkata_Raya) and obtained a grant for land on the Coromandel coast for their trading activities.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] A year later, the company built [Fort St. George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_St._George), the first major English settlement in India, which became the nucleus of the [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj) in the region.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] By 1693, the [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_people) established trading posts at [Pondichéry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pondich%C3%A9ry). In September 1746, the French captured Madras during the [Battle of Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Madras).[^cite_note-68] The British regained control of Madras in 1749 through the [Treaty of Aix-la-Chapelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Aix-la-Chapelle_\(1748\)) and resisted a [French siege attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Madras) in 1759.[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] The British and French competed to expand the trade which led to [Battle of Wandiwash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Wandiwash) in 1760 as part of the [Seven Years' War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Years'_War).[^cite_note-71] The Nawabs of the Carnatic surrendered much of their territory to the British East India Company in the north and bestowed tax revenue collection rights in the South, which led to constant conflicts with the Palaiyakkarars known as the [Polygar Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygar_Wars). [Puli Thevar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puli_Thevar) was one of the earliest opponents, joined later by [Rani Velu Nachiyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rani_Velu_Nachiyar) of [Sivagangai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sivagangai) and [Kattabomman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kattabomman) of Panchalakurichi in the first series of Polygar wars.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] The [Maruthu brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maruthu_brothers) along with [Oomaithurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oomaithurai), the brother of Kattabomman, formed a coalition with [Dheeran Chinnamalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dheeran_Chinnamalai) and Kerala Varma [Pazhassi Raja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazhassi_Raja), which fought the British in the Second Polygar War.[^cite_note-74] In the later 18th century, the [Mysore kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mysore_kingdom) captured parts of the region and engaged in constant fighting with the British which culminated in the four [Anglo-Mysore Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Mysore_War).[^cite_note-75]



An 18th-century coloured [engraving](60125.md) of [Fort St. George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_St._George) and Madras

By the 18th century, the British had conquered most of the region and established the [Madras Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Presidency) with Madras as the capital.[^cite_note-76] After the defeat of Mysore in the [Fourth Anglo-Mysore War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Anglo-Mysore_War) in 1799 and the British victory in the second Polygar war in 1801, the British consolidated most of southern India into what was later known as the Madras Presidency.[^cite_note-77] On 10 July 1806, the [Vellore mutiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vellore_mutiny), which was the first instance of a large-scale mutiny by Indian sepoys against the British East India Company, took place in [Vellore Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vellore_Fort).[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] After the [Indian Rebellion of 1857](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rebellion_of_1857), the [British Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Parliament) passed the [Government of India Act 1858](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India_Act_1858), which transferred the governance of India from the East India Company to the British crown, forming the [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj).[^cite_note-80]

Failure of the summer monsoons and administrative shortcomings of the [Ryotwari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryotwari) system resulted in two severe famines in the Madras Presidency, the [Great Famine of 1876–1878](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_of_1876%E2%80%931878) and the [Indian famine of 1896–1897](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_famine_of_1896%E2%80%931897) which killed millions and the migration of many Tamils as bonded labourers to other British countries eventually forming the present [Tamil diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_diaspora).[^cite_note-81] The [Indian Independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Independence_movement) gathered momentum in the early 20th century with the formation of the [Indian National Congress](149333.md), which was based on an idea propagated by the members of the [Theosophical Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theosophical_Society) movement after a Theosophical convention held in Madras in December 1884.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] Tamil Nadu was the base of various contributors to the Independence movement including [V. O. Chidambaram Pillai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._O._Chidambaram_Pillai), [Subramaniya Siva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subramaniya_Siva) and [Bharatiyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatiyar).[^cite_note-84] The Tamils formed a significant percentage of the members of the [Indian National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_National_Army) (INA), founded by [Subhas Chandra Bose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subhas_Chandra_Bose).[^cite_note-85]

### Post-Independence (1947–present)

After the [Independence of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_India) in 1947, the Madras Presidency became [Madras state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_state), comprising present-day Tamil Nadu and parts of [Andhra Pradesh](2377.md), [Karnataka](16880.md) and [Kerala](4349459.md). [Andhra state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_state) was split from the state in 1953 and the state was further reorganised when states were redrawn linguistically in [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Act,_1956) into the current shape.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87] On 14 January 1969, Madras state was renamed Tamil Nadu, meaning "Tamil country".[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] In 1965, [agitations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Hindi_agitations) against the [imposition of Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imposition_of_Hindi) and in support of continuing English as a medium of communication arose which eventually led to English being retained as an official language of India alongside Hindi.[^cite_note-90] After independence, the economy of Tamil Nadu conformed to a [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist) framework, with strict governmental control over [private sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_sector) participation, [foreign trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_trade), and [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment). After experiencing fluctuations in the decades immediately after Indian independence, the economy of Tamil Nadu consistently exceeded national average growth rates from the 1970s, due to [reform-oriented](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_reforms_in_India) economic policies.[^cite_note-91] In the 2000s, the state has become one of the most urbanised states in the country with a higher [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_Human_Development_Index) compared to national average.[^cite_note-DG-92]

## Environment

### Geography

Main article: [Geography of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Tamil_Nadu)



Topographic map of Tamil Nadu

Tamil Nadu covers an area of 130,058 km<sup>2</sup> (50,216 sq mi) and is the tenth-largest state in India.[^cite_note-DG-92] Located on the south-eastern coast of the [Indian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_peninsula), Tamil Nadu is straddled by the [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats) and [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) in the west, the [Eastern Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ghats) in the north, the [Eastern Coastal Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Coastal_Plains) lining the [Bay of Bengal](47892.md) in the east, the [Gulf of Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar) and the [Palk Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palk_Strait) to the south-east, and the [Laccadive Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laccadive_Sea) at the southern [cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_\(geography\)) of the peninsula.[^cite_note-93] Politically, Tamil Nadu is bound by the Indian states of [Kerala](4349459.md), [Karnataka](16880.md), and [Andhra Pradesh](2377.md), and the [union territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_territory) of [Puducherry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puducherry_\(union_territory\)). It shares an international [maritime border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_border) with the [Northern Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Province,_Sri_Lanka) of [Sri Lanka](26750.md) at [Pamban Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamban_Island). The Palk Strait and the chain of low sandbars and islands known as [Rama's Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rama's_Bridge) separate the region from Sri Lanka, which lies off the southeastern coast.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] The southernmost tip of mainland India is at [Kanyakumari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanyakumari) where the Indian Ocean meets the Bay of Bengal and the Arabian Sea.[^cite_note-96]



[Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats) traverse along the western border of Tamil Nadu

The Western Ghats run south along the western boundary with the highest peak at [Doddabetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doddabetta) (2,636 m (8,648 ft)) in the [Nilgiri Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Hills).[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] The [Eastern Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ghats) run parallel to the Bay of Bengal along the eastern coast and the strip of land between them forms the [Coromandel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coromandel_Coast) region.[^cite_note-99] They are a discontinuous range of mountains intersected by [Kaveri river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaveri_river).[^cite_note-100] Both mountain ranges meet at the [Nilgiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiris_\(mountains\)) mountains which run in a crescent approximately along the borders of Tamil Nadu with northern Kerala and Karnataka, extending to the relatively low-lying hills of the Eastern Ghats on the western portion of the Tamil Nadu–Andhra Pradesh border.[^cite_note-101] The [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) is the elevated region bound by the mountain ranges and the plateau slopes gently from west to east resulting in major rivers arising in the Western Ghats and flowing east into the Bay of Bengal.[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104] The [coastline of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastline_of_Tamil_Nadu) is 1,076 km (669 mi) long, and is the second longest state [coastline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastline) in the country after [Gujarat](53707.md).[^cite_note-Info-105] There are [coral reefs](87410.md) located in the Gulf of Mannar and Lakshadweep islands.[^cite_note-106] Tamil Nadu's coastline was permanently altered by the [Indian Ocean tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Ocean_tsunami) in 2004.[^cite_note-107]

### Geology

Tamil Nadu falls mostly in a region of low seismic hazard with the exception of the western border areas that lie in a low to moderate hazard zone; as per the 2002 Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS) map, Tamil Nadu falls in Zones II and III.[^cite_note-108] The volcanic [basalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basalt) beds of the Deccan plateau were laid down in the massive [Deccan Traps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Traps) eruption, which occurred towards the end of the [Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretaceous) period, between 67 and 66 million years ago.[^cite_note-109] Layer after layer was formed by the volcanic activity that lasted many years and when the volcanoes became extinct, they left a region of highlands with typically vast stretches of flat areas on top like a table.[^cite_note-110] The predominant soils of Tamil Nadu are [red loam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_soil), [laterite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laterite), [black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chernozem), [alluvial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluvial) and [saline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saline_soil). Red soil, with a higher iron content, occupies a larger portion of the state and all the inland districts. Black soil is found in [western Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kongu_Nadu) and parts of the southern coast. Alluvial soil is found in the fertile Kaveri delta region, with laterite soil found in pockets, and saline soil across the coast where the evaporation is high.[^cite_note-111]

### Climate



Climatic zones of India

The region has a [tropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_climate) and depends on monsoons for rainfall.[^cite_note-112] Tamil Nadu is divided into seven agro-climatic zones: northeast, northwest, west, southern, high rainfall, high altitude hilly, and [Kaveri](177299.md) delta.[^cite_note-113] A [tropical wet and dry climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_wet_and_dry_climate) prevails over most of the inland peninsular region except for a semi-arid [rain shadow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_shadow) east of the Western Ghats. Winter and early summer are long dry periods with temperatures averaging above 18 °C (64 °F); summer is exceedingly hot with temperatures in low-lying areas exceeding 50 °C (122 °F); and the rainy season lasts from June to September, with annual rainfall averaging between 750 and 1,500 mm (30 and 59 in) across the region. Once the dry northeast monsoon begins in September, most precipitation in India falls in Tamil Nadu, leaving other states comparatively dry.[^cite_note-114]



Tamil Nadu gets most of the rains from the [monsoon](57630.md)

A [hot semi-arid climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_semi-arid_climate) predominates in the land east of the Western Ghats which includes inland south and south central parts of the state and gets between 400 and 750 millimetres (15.7 and 29.5 in) of rainfall annually, with hot summers and dry winters with temperatures around 20–24 °C (68–75 °F). The months between March and May are hot and dry, with mean monthly temperatures hovering around 32 °C (90 °F), with 320 millimetres (13 in) precipitation. Without artificial irrigation, this region is not suitable for agriculture.[^cite_note-115]

The [southwest monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_monsoon) from June to September accounts for most of the rainfall in the west of the region. The Arabian Sea branch of the southwest monsoon hits the Western Ghats from Kerala and moves northward along the [Konkan coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan_coast), with precipitation on the western region of the state.[^cite_note-116] The lofty Western Ghats prevent the winds from reaching the Deccan Plateau; hence, the leeward region (the region deprived of winds) receives very little rainfall.[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] The Bay of Bengal branch of the southwest monsoon heads toward northeast India, picking up moisture from the Bay of Bengal. The Coramandel coast does not receive much rainfall from the southwest monsoon, due to the shape of the land.[^cite_note-119] The northern and eastern parts of Tamil Nadu receive most of their rains from the [northeast monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_monsoon).[^cite_note-120]

The northeast monsoon takes place from November to early March, when the surface [high-pressure system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-pressure_system) is strongest.[^cite_note-121] The [North Indian Ocean tropical cyclones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Indian_Ocean_tropical_cyclone) occur throughout the year in the Bay of Bengal and the Arabian Sea, bringing devastating winds and heavy rainfall.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] The annual rainfall of the state is about 945 mm (37.2 in) of which 48 per cent is through the northeast monsoon, and 52 per cent through the southwest monsoon. The state has only 3% of the water resources nationally and is entirely dependent on rains for recharging its water resources. Monsoon failures lead to acute [water scarcity](15380061.md) and [severe drought](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Drought_in_Tamil_Nadu).[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125]

### Flora and fauna

Main articles: [Wildlife of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Tamil_Nadu) and [List of birds of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Tamil_Nadu)



Tamil Nadu has one of the largest population of [Asian elephants](379035.md)

Forests occupy an area of 22,643 km<sup>2</sup> (8,743 sq mi) constituting 17.4% of the geographic area.[^cite_note-TNAU-126] There is a wide diversity of plants and animals in Tamil Nadu, resulting from its varied climates and geography. [Deciduous forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Western_Ghats_moist_deciduous_forests) are found along the Western Ghats while [tropical dry forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Deccan_Plateau_dry_deciduous_forests) and [scrub lands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_thorn_scrub_forests) are common in the interior.[^cite_note-127] The southern Western Ghats have rain forests located at high altitudes called the [South Western Ghats montane rain forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Western_Ghats_montane_rain_forests).[^cite_note-128] The Western Ghats eco-region is one of the eight hottest [biodiversity hotspots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot) in the world and a UNESCO [World Heritage Site](44940.md).[^cite_note-129] There are about 2,000 species of wildlife that are native to Tamil Nadu, 5,640 species of [angiosperms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angiosperm) (including 1,559 species of [medicinal plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicinal_plant), 533 [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic) species, 260 species of wild relatives of cultivated plants, 230 [red-listed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-listed) species), 64 species of [gymnosperms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymnosperm) (including four indigenous species and 60 introduced species) and 184 species of [pteridophytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pteridophyte) apart from [bryophytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryophyte), [lichen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lichen), [fungi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fungi), [algae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algae), and [bacteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacteria).[^cite_note-130] Common plant species include the state tree: [palmyra palm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borassus_flabellifer#Cultural_symbolism), [eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus), rubber, [cinchona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinchona), clumping bamboos (*[Bambusa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambusa) arundinacea*), [common teak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tectona_grandis), *[Anogeissus latifolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anogeissus_latifolia)*, [Indian laurel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminalia_tomentosa), [grewia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grewia), and blooming trees like [Indian laburnum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_laburnum), [ardisia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardisia), and [solanaceae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solanaceae). Rare and unique plant life includes *Combretum ovalifolium*, [ebony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebony) (*Diospyros nilagrica*), *[Habenaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habenaria) rariflora* (orchid), *[Alsophila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyathea_sect._Alsophila)*, *[Impatiens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impatiens) elegans*, *[Ranunculus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranunculus) reniformis*, and [royal fern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osmunda_regalis).[^cite_note-131]



[Nilgiri tahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_tahr), an [endangered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endangered) animal found only in the [Nilgiris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiris), is the state animal

Important ecological regions of Tamil Nadu are the [Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Biosphere_Reserve) in the [Nilgiri Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Hills), the [Agasthyamala Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agasthyamala_Biosphere_Reserve) in the [Agastya Mala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agastya_Mala)-[Cardamom Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardamom_Hills) and [Gulf of Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar_Marine_National_Park) coral reefs.[^cite_note-132] The [Gulf of Mannar Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar_Biosphere_Reserve) covers an area of 10,500 km<sup>2</sup> (4,100 sq mi) of ocean, islands and the adjoining coastline including [coral reefs](87410.md), [salt marshes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_marsh) and mangroves. It is home to [endangered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endangered) aquatic species, including [dolphins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolphin), [dugongs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dugong), [whales](33777.md) and [sea cucumbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_cucumbers).[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] Bird sanctuaries, including [Thattekad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thattekad), [Kadalundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadalundi_Bird_Sanctuary), [Vedanthangal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedanthangal), [Ranganathittu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranganathittu), [Kumarakom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumarakom_Bird_Sanctuary), [Neelapattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neelapattu_Sanctuary), and [Pulicat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulicat_Lake), are home to numerous migratory and local birds.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136][^cite_note-ENVIS-137]



[Bengal tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_tiger) at [Mudumalai National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mudumalai_National_Park)

Protected areas cover an area of 3,305 km<sup>2</sup> (1,276 sq mi), constituting 2.54% of the geographic area and 15% of the 22,643 km<sup>2</sup> (8,743 sq mi) recorded forest area of the state.[^cite_note-TNAU-126] [Vedanthangal Bird Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedanthangal_Bird_Sanctuary), established in 1936, was India's first [wildlife sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wildlife_sanctuaries_of_India).[^cite_note-138] [Mudumalai National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mudumalai_National_Park) was established later in 1940.[^cite_note-139] The protected areas are administered by the [Ministry of Environment and Forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Environment_and_Forests) of the government of India and the [Tamil Nadu Forest Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Forest_Department). [Pichavaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pichavaram) consists of a number of islands interspersing the [Vellar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vellar_River) estuary in the north and [Coleroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleroon) estuary in the south with [mangrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) forests. The Pichavaram mangrove forests is one of the largest mangrove forests in India covering 45 km<sup>2</sup> (17 sq mi) and supports the existence of rare varieties of economically important shells, fishes and migrant birds.[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141] The state has five [National Parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks_of_India) covering 307.84 km<sup>2</sup> (118.86 sq mi)–[Anamalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indira_Gandhi_National_Park), Mudumalai, [Mukurthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukurthi_National_Park), [Gulf of Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar_Marine_National_Park), a marine national park and [Guindy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guindy_National_Park), an urban national park within Chennai.[^cite_note-ENVIS-137] Tamil Nadu has 18 [wildlife sanctuaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_India#Wildlife_sanctuaries).[^cite_note-ENVIS-137][^cite_note-142] Tamil Nadu is home to one of the largest populations of endangered [Bengal tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_tigers) and [Indian elephants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_elephants) in India.[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] There are five declared elephant sanctuaries in Tamil Nadu as per [Project Elephant](12609307.md)–Agasthyamalai, [Anamalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anamalai_Tiger_Reserve), [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_Elephant_Reserve), Nilgiris and [Srivilliputtur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srivilliputhur-Megamalai_Tiger_Reserve).[^cite_note-ENVIS-137] Tamil Nadu participates in [Project Tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Tiger) and has five declared [tiger reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_reserves)–Anamalai, [Kalakkad-Mundanthurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalakkad_Mundanthurai_Tiger_Reserve), [Mudumalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mudumalai), [Sathyamangalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sathyamangalam_Wildlife_Sanctuary) and Megamalai.[^cite_note-ENVIS-137][^cite_note-MOEF-145][^cite_note-146] There are seventeen declared bird sanctuaries in Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-ENVIS-137][^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148]



[Mangrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) forests at [Pichavaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pichavaram)

There is one conservation reserve at [Tiruvidaimarudur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruvidaimarudur_Conservation_Reserve) in [Thanjavur district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur_district). There are two [zoos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_protected_areas_in_India#Zoos) recognised by the [Central Zoo Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Zoo_Authority_of_India) namely [Arignar Anna Zoological Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arignar_Anna_Zoological_Park) and [Madras Crocodile Bank Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Crocodile_Bank_Trust), both located in [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai).[^cite_note-ENVIS-137] The state has other smaller zoos run by local administrative bodies such as [Coimbatore Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VOC_park_and_zoo) in [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore), [Amirthi Zoological Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amirthi_Zoological_Park) in [Vellore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vellore), Kurumpampatti Wildlife Park in [Salem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_district), Yercaud Deer Park in [Yercaud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yercaud), Mukkombu Deer Park in [Tiruchirapalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruchirapalli_district) and Ooty Deer Park in [Nilgiris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiris_district).[^cite_note-ENVIS-137] There are five crocodile farms located at [Amaravati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravathi_Dam#Crocodiles) in [Coimbatore district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_district), [Hogenakkal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hogenakkal_\(village\)) in [Dharmapuri district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharmapuri_district), Kurumbapatti in [Salem district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_district), Madras Crocodile Bank Trust in Chennai and [Sathanur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sathanur_Reservoir) in [Tiruvannamalai district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruvannamalai_district).[^cite_note-ENVIS-137] Threatened and endangered species found in the region include the [grizzled giant squirrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grizzled_giant_squirrel),[^cite_note-149] [grey slender loris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_slender_loris),[^cite_note-150] [sloth bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloth_bear),[^cite_note-151] [Nilgiri tahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_tahr),[^cite_note-152] [Nilgiri langur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_langur),[^cite_note-153] [lion-tailed macaque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion-tailed_macaque),[^cite_note-154] and the [Indian leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_leopard).[^cite_note-155]

| Animal | Bird | Butterfly | Tree | Fruit | Flower |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Nilgiri tahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_tahr) (*Nilgiritragus hylocrius*) | [Emerald dove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerald_dove) (*Chalcophaps indica*) | [Tamil Yeoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Yeoman) (*Cirrochroa thais*) | [Palmyra palm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borassus_flabellifer) (*Borassus flabellifer*) | [Jackfruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackfruit) (*Artocarpus heterophyllus*) | [Glory lily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloriosa_superba) (*Gloriosa superba*) |

## Administration and politics

### Administration

| Title | Name |
| --- | --- |
| [Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Tamil_Nadu) | [Rajendra Arlekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajendra_Arlekar)[^cite_note-158] |
| [Chief minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Tamil_Nadu) | [C. Joseph Vijay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._Joseph_Vijay)[^cite_note-159] |
| [Chief Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_former_chief_justices_of_the_high_courts_of_India#Madras_High_Court) | [S. A. Dharmadhikari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._A._Dharmadhikari)[^cite_note-160] |

Further information: [Local government in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Tamil_Nadu) and [List of departments of the government of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_departments_of_the_government_of_Tamil_Nadu)

Chennai is the capital of the state and houses the [state executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Tamil_Nadu), [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative) and [head of judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Courts_of_India).[^cite_note-161] The administration of the state government functions through various secretariat departments. There are 43 departments of the state and the departments have further sub-divisions which may govern various undertakings and boards.[^cite_note-162] The state is divided into 38 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Tamil_Nadu), each of which is administered by a [District Collector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Collector), who is an officer of the [Indian Administrative Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Administrative_Service) (IAS) appointed to the district by the [Government of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Tamil_Nadu). For land revenue administration, the districts are further subdivided into 87 revenue divisions administered by [Revenue Divisional Officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-divisional_magistrate) (RDO) which comprise 310 [taluks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taluks) administered by [Tehsildars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehsildar).[^cite_note-AS-163] The taluks are divided into 1349 [revenue blocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenue_block) called *[Firkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firka)* which consist of 17,680 [revenue villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenue_Village).[^cite_note-AS-163]

The [local administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_India) consists of a single tier system for urban areas, which are governed by either of the three types of urban bodies–[municipal corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_corporation), [municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality), or [town panchayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_panchayat), based on revenue and population.[^cite_note-PR-164] As of 2026, there are 25 [municipal corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Municipal_Corporations_of_Tamil_Nadu), 138 [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Municipalities_of_Tamil_Nadu) and 528 town panchayats in the state.[^cite_note-LG-165][^cite_note-AS-163] The [Greater Chennai Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Chennai_Corporation), established in 1688, is the second oldest in the world and [^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] Tamil Nadu was the first state to establish town panchayats as a new administrative unit.[^cite_note-PR-164][^cite_note-LG-165] The rural administration consists of a three-tier [Panchayati Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayati_raj_in_India) system with the [district panchayats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_council_\(India\)) at the district level, [panchayat unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayat_samiti) at the intermediate (block) level, and [village panchayats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gram_panchayat) at the village level.[^cite_note-PR-164] As of 2026, there are 36 district panchayats, 288 panchayat unions and 12,525 village panchayats, administered by village administrative officers.[^cite_note-LG-165][^cite_note-AS-163][^cite_note-168]

### Legislature



[Fort St. George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_St._George) is the seat of the [Tamil Nadu Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Legislative_Assembly)

In accordance with the [Constitution of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_India), the [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Tamil_Nadu) is a state's *[de jure](43594.md)* head and appoints the [chief minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Tamil_Nadu) who has the *[de facto](43594.md)* executive authority.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170] The [Indian Councils Act 1861](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Councils_Act_1861) established the Madras Presidency legislative council with four to eight members but was a mere advisory body to the governor of the presidency. The strength was increased to twenty in [1892](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Councils_Act_of_1892) and fifty in [1909](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Councils_Act_1909).[^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172] [Madras legislative council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Legislative_Council) was set up in 1921 by the [Government of India Act 1919](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India_Act_1919) with a term of three years and consisted of 132 Members of which 34 were nominated by the Governor and the rest were elected.[^cite_note-SL-173] The [Government of India Act 1935](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India_Act_1935) established a [bicameral legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral_legislature) with the creation of a new [legislative council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Legislative_Council) with 54 to 56 members in July 1937.[^cite_note-SL-173]

The [first legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Madras_Assembly) of Madras state under the [Constitution of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_India) was constituted on 1 March 1952 after the [1952 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_Madras_Legislative_Assembly_election). The number of seats post the reorganisation in 1956 was 206, which was further increased to 234 in 1962.[^cite_note-SL-173] In 1986, the state moved to a [unicameral legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unicameral_legislature) with the abolition of the Legislative Council by the Tamil Nadu Legislative Council (Abolition) act, 1986.[^cite_note-174] The Tamil Nadu Legislative Assembly is housed in the [Fort St. George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_St._George) in Chennai.[^cite_note-175] The state elects 39 [members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_\(India\)) to the [Lok Sabha](275456.md) and 18 to the [Rajya Sabha](232210.md) of the [Indian Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Parliament).[^cite_note-176]

### Law and order



[Madras High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_High_Court), the highest judicial authority in the state

The [Madras High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_High_Court) was established on 26 June 1862 and is the highest judicial authority of the state with control over all the civil and criminal courts in the state.[^cite_note-HC-177] It is headed by a [Chief Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice) and has a bench at Madurai since 2004.[^cite_note-HC-177]

The [Tamil Nadu Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Police), established as Madras state police in 1859, operates under the [home ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Home,_Prohibition_and_Excise) of the Government of Tamil Nadu and is responsible for maintaining law and order in the state.[^cite_note-178] As of 2023, it consists of more than 132,000 police personnel, headed by a [Director General of Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director_General_of_Police).[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] Women form 17.6% of the police force and specifically handle [violence against women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violence_against_women_in_Tamil_Nadu) through 222 special all-women police stations.[^cite_note-PS-181][^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183] As of 2023, the state has 1854 police stations, the highest in the country, including 47 railway and 243 traffic police stations.[^cite_note-PS-181][^cite_note-184] The traffic police under different district administrations are responsible for the traffic management in the respective regions.[^cite_note-185] The state is consistently ranked as one of the [safest for women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_states_ranking_by_safety_of_women) with a crime rate of 22 per 100,000 in 2018.[^cite_note-186]

### Politics

Main articles: [Elections in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Tamil_Nadu) and [Politics of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Tamil_Nadu)



[K. Kamaraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._Kamaraj) served as the [chief minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Tamil_Nadu) from 1954 to 1963

Elections in India are conducted by the [Election Commission of India](47863083.md), an independent body established in 1950.[^cite_note-187] Politics in Tamil Nadu was dominated by national parties till the 1960s. Regional parties have ruled ever since. The [Justice Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Party_\(India\)) and [Swaraj Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swaraj_Party) were the two major parties in the erstwhile Madras Presidency.[^cite_note-encyclopp-188] During the 1920s and 1930s, the [Self-Respect Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-Respect_Movement), spearheaded by [Theagaroya Chetty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theagaroya_Chetty) and [E. V. Ramaswamy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E._V._Ramaswamy) (commonly known as Periyar), emerged in the Madras Presidency and led to the formation of the Justice party.[^cite_note-189] The Justice Party eventually lost the [1937 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1937_Madras_Presidency_legislative_assembly_election) to the [Indian National Congress](149333.md) and [Chakravarti Rajagopalachari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chakravarti_Rajagopalachari) became the chief minister of the Madras Presidency.[^cite_note-encyclopp-188] In 1944, Periyar transformed the Justice party into a social organisation, renaming the party [Dravidar Kazhagam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidar_Kazhagam), and withdrew from electoral politics.[^cite_note-190]



[APJ Abdul Kalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APJ_Abdul_Kalam) served as the [eleventh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_presidents_of_India) [President of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_India)

After independence, Rajagopalachari served as the last [Governor General of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_General_of_India) before India became a republic.[^cite_note-191] [C. N. Annadurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._N._Annadurai), a follower of Periyar, formed the [Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravida_Munnetra_Kazhagam) (DMK) in 1949.[^cite_note-192] The Congress dominated the political scene in Tamil Nadu in the 1950s and 1960s under the leadership of [K. Kamaraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._Kamaraj), who led the party after the death of [Jawaharlal Nehru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru) and ensured the selection of [Lal Bahadur Shastri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri) and [Indira Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indira_Gandhi) as [prime ministers](24452.md).[^cite_note-CM-193][^cite_note-194]

The [anti-Hindi agitations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Hindi_agitations) led to the rise of [Dravidian parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_parties) and the first such government was formed in [1967](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967_Madras_State_Legislative_Assembly_election).[^cite_note-195] In 1972, a split in the DMK resulted in the formation of the [All India Anna Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Anna_Dravida_Munnetra_Kazhagam) (AIADMK) led by [M. G. Ramachandran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._G._Ramachandran).[^cite_note-196] These two Dravidian parties continued to dominate the electoral politics in the state with the national parties often aligning as junior partners.[^cite_note-197] [M. Karunanidhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._Karunanidhi), who became the leader of the DMK after Annadurai and [J. Jayalalithaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Jayalalithaa), who succeeded as the leader of AIADMK after M. G. Ramachandran dominated the state politics from the 1980s to early 2010s, serving as chief ministers combined for over 32 years.[^cite_note-CM-193][^cite_note-198]

The state has produced three [Indian presidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_India), namely, [Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarvepalli_Radhakrishnan),[^cite_note-199] [R. Venkataraman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R._Venkataraman),[^cite_note-200] and [APJ Abdul Kalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APJ_Abdul_Kalam).[^cite_note-201]

## Demographics

| Year | Pop. | ±% |
| --- | --- | --- |

| 1911 | 20,902,616 | +8.6% |
| 1921 | 21,628,518 | +3.5% |

Main article: [Demographics of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Tamil_Nadu)

As per the [2011 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Census_of_India), Tamil Nadu had a population of 72.1 million and is the [seventh most populous state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_population) in India.[^cite_note-pop-1] The population is projected to be 76.8 million in 2023 and to grow to 78 million by 2036.[^cite_note-202] Tamil Nadu is one of the most urbanised states in the country with more than 48.4 per cent of the population living in urban areas.[^cite_note-DG-92] As per the 2011 census, the [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_ratio) was 996 females per 1000 males, higher than the national average of 943.[^cite_note-SR-203] The sex ratio at birth was recorded as 954 during the fourth [National Family Health Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Family_Health_Survey) (NFHS) in 2015–16 which reduced further to 878 in the fifth NFHS in 2019–21, ranking [third worst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_sex_ratio) amongst states.[^cite_note-204] As per the 2011 census, [Literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_literacy_rate) was 80.1%, higher than the national average of 73%.[^cite_note-LR-205] The literacy rate was estimated to be 82.9% as per the 2017 [National Statistical Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Statistical_Commission) (NSC) survey.[^cite_note-NSC-206] As of 2011, there were about 23.17 million households with 7.42 million children under the age of six.[^cite_note-207] A total of 14.4 million (20%) belonged to [Scheduled Castes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheduled_Castes) (SC) and 0.8 million (1.1%) to [Scheduled tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheduled_tribes) (ST).[^cite_note-208]

As of 2017, the state had the [lowest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_states_ranking_by_fertility_rate) [fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertility_rate) in India with 1.6 children born for each woman, lower than required for sustaining the population.[^cite_note-209] As of 2022, Tamil Nadu had a [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) (HDI) of 0.692, higher than that of the overall HDI of the country (0.644) and [ranked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_Human_Development_Index) 14th amongst the Indian states and territories.[^cite_note-HDI-210] As of 2019, the [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) at birth was 74 years, one of the [highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_by_life_expectancy_at_birth) amongst Indian states.[^cite_note-LE-211] As of 2023, 2.2% of the people live below the [poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_line) as per the [Multidimensional Poverty Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multidimensional_Poverty_Index), one of the lowest rates amongst Indian states.[^cite_note-PL-212]

### Cities and towns

Main article: [List of cities in Tamil Nadu by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Tamil_Nadu_by_population)

Further information: [List of towns in Tamil Nadu by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_towns_in_Tamil_Nadu_by_population)
