### Religion and ethnicity

| Religion |  | Percent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Tamil_Nadu) |  | 87.6% |
| [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Tamil_Nadu) |  | 6.1% |
| [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Muslim) |  | 5.86% |
| Other or not stated |  | 0.4% |

Main article: [Religion in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Tamil_Nadu)

The state is home to a diverse population of ethno-religious communities.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] According to the 2011 census, [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Tamil_Nadu) is followed by 87.6% of the population. [Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Tamil_Nadu) form the largest [religious minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_minority) in the state with 6.1% of the population; [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Muslim) form 5.9% of the population.[^cite_note-RL-217]

[Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamils) form a majority of the population with minorities including [Telugus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugus),[^cite_note-218] [Marwaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwaris),[^cite_note-219] [Gujaratis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujaratis),[^cite_note-220] [Parsis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsi),[^cite_note-221] [Sindhis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sindhis),[^cite_note-222] [Odias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odias),[^cite_note-223] [Kannadigas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannadigas),[^cite_note-224] [Anglo-Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Indians),[^cite_note-225] [Bengalis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengalis),[^cite_note-226] [Punjabis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjabis),[^cite_note-227] and [Malayalees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalees).[^cite_note-228] The state also has a significant [expatriate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expatriate) population.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] As of 2011, the state had 3.49 million immigrants.[^cite_note-231]

### Language

Distribution of languages in Tamil Nadu (2011)

[^cite_note-LRT-232]

1. [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) (88.3%)

2. [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) (5.87%)

3. [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada) (1.78%)

4. [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) (1.75%)

5. [Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam) (1.01%)

6. Others (1.24%)

Tamil is the official language of Tamil Nadu, while [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) serves as the additional official language.[^cite_note-Lang-3] Tamil is one of the oldest languages and was the first to be recognised as a [classical language of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_language_of_India).[^cite_note-233] As per the 2011 census, Tamil is spoken as the first language by 88.4% of the state's population, followed by [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) (5.87%), [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada) (1.78%), [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) (1.75%), [Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam) (1.01%) and other languages (1.24%)[^cite_note-LRT-232] Various [varieties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_\(linguistics\)) of Tamil are spoken across regions such as [Madras Bashai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Bashai) in northern Tamil Nadu, [Kongu Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kongu_Tamil) in [Western Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kongu_Nadu), [Madurai Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurai_Tamil) around Madurai and [Nellai Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nellai_Tamil) in South-eastern Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] It is part of the [Dravidian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_languages) and preserves many features of [Proto-Dravidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Dravidian), though modern-day spoken Tamil in Tamil Nadu freely uses [loanwords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loanword) from other languages such as [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit) and English.[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237] [Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_language),[^cite_note-238] [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Language),[^cite_note-239] [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language),[^cite_note-FP-240] [Mandarin Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese),[^cite_note-241] [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language)[^cite_note-242] and [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Language) are spoken by foreign expatriates in the state.[^cite_note-FP-240]

### LGBT rights

Main article: [LGBT rights in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Tamil_Nadu)

The [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) rights in Tamil Nadu are among the most progressive in [India](14533.md).[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] In 2008, Tamil Nadu set up the Transgender welfare board and was the first to introduce a transgender welfare policy, wherein transgender people can avail free [sex reassignment surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_reassignment_surgery) in government hospitals.[^cite_note-245] [Chennai Rainbow Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Rainbow_Pride) has been held in Chennai annually since 2009.[^cite_note-246] In 2021, Tamil Nadu became the first Indian state to ban conversion therapy and [forced sex-selective surgeries on intersex infants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersex_medical_interventions), following the directions of the Madras High Court.[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] In 2019, the Madras High Court ruled that the term "bride" under the [Hindu Marriage Act, 1955](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Marriage_Act,_1955) includes trans-women, thereby legalising marriage between a man and a transgender woman.[^cite_note-250]

## Culture and heritage

Further information: [Tamil culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_culture)

### Clothing



[Kanchipuram silk saris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram_silk_sari) worn by women on special occasions

Tamil women traditionally wear a *[sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari)*, a garment measuring 15 to 27 ft (4.6 to 8.2 m) in length and 2 to 4 ft (0.61 to 1.22 m) in breadth that is typically wrapped around the waist, with one end draped over the shoulder, baring the midriff, as according to [Indian philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_philosophy), the navel is considered as the source of life and creativity.[^cite_note-Drape-251][^cite_note-252] Ancient Tamil poetry such as the *Cilappadhikaram*, describes women in exquisite drapery or sari.[^cite_note-253] Women wear colourful [silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk) saris on special occasions such as marriages.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] The [Kanchipuram silk sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram_silk_sari) is a type of silk sari made in the [Kanchipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram) region in Tamil Nadu and these saris are worn as a part of the bridal attire and during special occasions. It has been recognised as a [Geographical indication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_indication) by the Government of India in 2005–2006.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-GI-257] [Kovai Cora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kovai_Cora_Cotton) is a type of [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton) sari made in the Coimbatore.[^cite_note-GI-257][^cite_note-258] Young girls wear a long skirt called *pavaadai* along with a long over cloth called *dhavani*.[^cite_note-259]

The men wear a *[veshti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veshti)*, a rectangular piece of non-stitched cloth, of varying lengths, that is wrapped around the legs and knotted at the waist. It is often white, bordered in brightly coloured stripes.[^cite_note-Dress-260] The style of draping might vary between communities, and men sometimes wear a hip belt to hold the veshti in place.[^cite_note-Drape-251] Traditional attire includes a *[dhoti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhoti)*, a costume similar to veshti, which is a long, white rectangular piece of non-stitched cloth, that is wrapped around the waist, looped around the legs (similar to trousers) and knotted at the waist.[^cite_note-261] A *[lungi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lungi)*, which is similar to a veshti but made tubular with the ends stitched together, designed with colourful [batik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batik) patterns, is a common form of male attire in the countryside.[^cite_note-Dress-260][^cite_note-Cloth-262] People in urban areas generally wear tailored clothing, and western dress is popular. Western-style school uniforms are worn by both boys and girls in schools, even in rural areas.[^cite_note-Cloth-262]

### Cuisine



Traditional meal served on a [banana leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_leaf)

[Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the diet staple and is served with *[sambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambar_\(dish\))*, *[rasam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasam_\(dish\))*, and *[poriyal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poriyal)* as a part of a Tamil meal.[^cite_note-263] [Coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut) and spices are used extensively in Tamil cuisine. The region has a rich cuisine involving both traditional non-vegetarian and vegetarian dishes made of rice, [legumes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legume), and [lentils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lentil) with its distinct aroma and flavour achieved by the blending of [flavourings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flavouring) and spices.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] The traditional way of eating a meal involves being seated on the floor, having the food served on a [banana leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_leaf),[^cite_note-266] and using clean fingers of the right hand to take the food into the mouth.[^cite_note-267] After the meal, the fingers are washed; the easily degradable banana leaf is discarded or becomes fodder for cattle.[^cite_note-268] Eating on banana leaves is a custom thousands of years old, imparts a unique flavour to the food, and is considered healthy.[^cite_note-269] [Idli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idli), [dosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dosa_\(food\)), [uthappam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uthappam), [pongal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pongal_\(dish\)), and [paniyaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paniyaram) are popular breakfast dishes in Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-270] [Palani Panchamirtham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palani_Panchamirtham), [Ooty varkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ooty_varkey), [Kovilpatti Kadalai Mittai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chikki), [Manapparai Murukku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manapparai_Murukku) and [Srivilliputhur Palkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khoa) are unique foods that have been recognised as [Geographical Indications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_geographical_indications_in_India).[^cite_note-271]

### Literature

Further information: [Tamil Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Literature)



Sculpture of sage [Agastya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agastya)

Tamil Nadu has an independent [literary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_literature) tradition dating back over 2500 years from the Sangam era.[^cite_note-Zvelebil-8] Early Tamil literature was composed in three successive poetic assemblies known as the [Tamil Sangams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Sangams), the earliest of which, according to legend, were held on a now vanished [continent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumari_Kandam) far to the south of India.[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] This includes the oldest grammatical treatise, *[Tolkappiyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tolkappiyam)*, and the epics *Cilappatikaram* and *[Manimekalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manimekalai)*.[^cite_note-275] The earliest [epigraphic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epigraphic) records found on rock edicts and *[hero stones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hero_stone)* date from around the 3rd century BCE.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] The available literature from the Sangam period was categorised and compiled into two categories based roughly on chronology: the [Patiṉeṇmēlkaṇakku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pati%E1%B9%89e%E1%B9%87m%C4%93lka%E1%B9%87akku) consisting of [Eṭṭuttokai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E%E1%B9%AD%E1%B9%ADuttokai) and the [Pattupattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pattupattu), and the [Patiṉeṇkīḻkaṇakku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pati%E1%B9%89e%E1%B9%87k%C4%AB%E1%B8%BBka%E1%B9%87akku). The existent Tamil grammar is largely based on the 13th-century grammar book *_Naṉṉūl_* based on the *Tolkāppiyam.* Tamil grammar consists of five parts, namely *_eḻuttu_*, *sol*, *_poruḷ_*, *yāppu*, *_aṇi_*.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] *[Tirukkural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirukkural)*, a book on ethics by [Thiruvalluvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvalluvar), is amongst the most popular works of Tamil literature.[^cite_note-280]

The Tamil literature that followed in the next 300 years after the Sangam period is generally called the "post-Sangam" literature which included the [Five Great Epics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Great_Epics) and the [Five Minor Epics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Minor_Epics).[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] In the early medieval period, [Vaishnava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaishnava) and [Shaiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saiva) literature became prominent following the [Bhakti movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhakti_movement) in the sixth century CE with hymns composed by the [Alvars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvars) and the [Nayanars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayanars).[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286] In the following years, Tamil literature again flourished with notable works including [Ramavataram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramavataram), written in the 12th century CE by [Kambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kambar_\(poet\)).[^cite_note-287] After a lull in the intermediate years due to various invasions and instability, the Tamil literature recovered in the 14th century CE, with the notable work being *[Tiruppukal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruppukal)* by [Arunagirinathar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arunagirinathar).[^cite_note-288] In 1578, the Portuguese published a Tamil book in old Tamil script named *Thambiraan Vanakkam*, thus making Tamil the first Indian language to be printed and published.[^cite_note-289] *[Tamil Lexicon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Lexicon)*, published by the [University of Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Madras), is the first among the [dictionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictionaries) published in any Indian language.[^cite_note-290] The 19th century gave rise to the [Tamil Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Renaissance) and writings and poems by authors such as [Meenakshi Sundaram Pillai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meenakshi_Sundaram_Pillai), [U.V. Swaminatha Iyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.V._Swaminatha_Iyer), [Ramalinga Swamigal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramalinga_Swamigal), [Maraimalai Adigal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maraimalai_Adigal), and [Bharathidasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharathidasan).[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] During the [Indian Independence Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Independence_Movement), many Tamil poets and writers sought to provoke national spirit, social equity and secularist thoughts, notably [Subramania Bharati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subramania_Bharati) and Bharathidasan.[^cite_note-293]

### Architecture

Further information: [Architecture of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Tamil_Nadu)



The large *[gopuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram)* is a hallmark of [Dravidian architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_architecture)

[Dravidian architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_architecture) is the distinct style of rock architecture in Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-Hindu-294] In Dravidian architecture, the temples consisted of porches or *mantapas* preceding the door leading to the sanctum, gate-pyramids or *[gopurams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram)* in quadrangular enclosures that surround the temple, and *pillared halls* used for many purposes. These features are the invariable accompaniments of these temples. Besides these, a South Indian temple usually has a [tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_tank) called the *kalyani* or *pushkarni*.[^cite_note-295] The gopuram is a monumental tower, usually ornate at the entrance of the temple forms a prominent feature of [koils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koil) and [Hindu temples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_temple_architecture) of the Dravidian style.[^cite_note-Gopuram-296] They are topped by the *kalasam*, a bulbous stone [finial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finial) and function as gateways through the walls that surround the temple complex.[^cite_note-297] The gopuram's origins can be traced back to the [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava_art_and_architecture) who built the group of monuments in [Mahabalipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabalipuram) and [Kanchipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram).[^cite_note-UNC-37] The [Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_art_and_architecture) later expanded the same and by the [Pandya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandyan_art_and_architecture) rule in twelfth century, these gateways became a dominant feature of a temple's outer appearance.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-Brit-299] The [state emblem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emblem_of_Tamil_Nadu) also features the [Lion Capital of Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion_Capital_of_Ashoka) with an image of a Gopuram on the background.[^cite_note-300] *[Vimanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vimana_\(architectural_feature\))* are similar structures built over the *[garbhagriha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbhagriha)* or inner sanctum of the temple but are usually smaller than the gopurams in the Dravidian architecture with a few exceptions including the [Brihadisvara Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihadisvara_Temple) in [Thanjavur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur).[^cite_note-301]

The [Mugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugal_Empire) influence in medieval times and the [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) influence later gave rise to a blend of [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu), [Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic) and [Gothic revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_revival) styles, resulting in the distinct [Indo-Saracenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Saracenic) architecture. Several buildings and institutions built during the British era followed the style.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] By the early 20th century, [art deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_deco) made its entry in the urban landscape.[^cite_note-304] After Indian Independence, Tamil architecture witnessed a rise in [Modernism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernism) with the transition from lime-and-brick construction to [concrete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concrete) columns.[^cite_note-305]

### Arts

Further information: [Dance forms of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_forms_of_Tamil_Nadu)



The [classical dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_classical_dance) [Bharatanatyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatanatyam) is one of the oldest dance forms of India

Tamil Nadu is a major centre for music, art and dance in India.[^cite_note-306] Chennai is called the cultural capital of South India.[^cite_note-307] In the Sangam era, art forms were classified into: *iyal* (poetry), *isai* (music) and *nadakam* (drama).[^cite_note-308] [Bharatanatyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatanatyam) is a classical dance form that originated in Tamil Nadu and is one of the oldest dances of India.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] Other regional folk dances include [Karakattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karakattam), [Kavadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavadi), [Koodiyattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koodiyattam), [Oyilattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oyilattam), [Paraiattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parai_Attam) and [Puravaiattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poikkaal_Kuthirai_Aattam).[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] The dance, clothing, and sculptures of Tamil Nadu exemplify the beauty of the body and motherhood.[^cite_note-316] [Koothu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koothu) is an ancient folk art, where artists tell stories from the epics accompanied by dance and music.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318]

The ancient Tamil country had its own [system of music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Tamil_music) called Tamil Pannisai described by Sangam literature such as the *Silappatikaram*.[^cite_note-319] A [Pallava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava) inscription dated to the 7th century CE has one of the earliest surviving examples of Indian music in notation.[^cite_note-320] There are many traditional instruments from the region dating back to the Sangam period such as [parai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parai), [tharai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tharai), [yazh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazh) and [murasu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murasu).[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] [Nadaswaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadaswaram), a [reed instrument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_instrument) that is often accompanied by the [thavil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thavil), a type of [drum instrument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_instrument), are the major musical instruments used in temples and weddings.[^cite_note-323] [Melam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melam) is a group of [madhalams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhalam) and other similar percussion instruments from the ancient Tamilakam which are played during events.[^cite_note-AA-324] The traditional music of Tamil Nadu is known as [Carnatic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_music), which includes rhythmic and structured music by composers such as [Muthuswami Dikshitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muthuswami_Dikshitar).[^cite_note-325] [Gaana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaana), a combination of various folk musics, is sung mainly in the working-class area of North Chennai.[^cite_note-326]



[Government Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Museum,_Chennai) in Chennai, the second oldest museum in India

The state is home to many museums, galleries, and other institutions which engage in arts research and are major tourist attractions.[^cite_note-327] Established in the early 18th century, the [Government Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Museum,_Chennai) and the [National Art Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Art_Gallery,_Chennai) are amongst the oldest in the country.[^cite_note-328] The museum inside the premises of Fort St. George maintains a collection of objects of the British era.[^cite_note-329] The museum is managed by the [Archaeological Survey of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Survey_of_India), and has in its possession the first [Flag of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_India) hoisted at [Fort St George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_St_George) after the declaration of India's Independence on [15 August 1947](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15_August_1947).[^cite_note-330]

Tamil Nadu is also home to the Tamil film industry nicknamed as "Kollywood" and is one of the largest industries of film production in India.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] The term *Kollywood* is a blend of [Kodambakkam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodambakkam) and Hollywood.[^cite_note-333] The first silent film in South India was produced in Tamil in 1916 and the first talkie was a multilingual film, *[Kalidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalidas_\(film\))*, which was released on 31 October 1931, barely seven months after India's first talking picture *[Alam Ara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alam_Ara)*.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] [Samikannu Vincent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samikannu_Vincent), who had built the first cinema of South India in Coimbatore, introduced the concept of "Tent Cinema" in which a tent was erected on a stretch of open land close to a town or village to screen the films. The first of its kind was established in Madras, called "Edison's Grand Cinemamegaphone".[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338]

### Festivals



[Jallikattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jallikattu), a traditional bull taming event held during [Pongal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pongal_\(festival\)) festivities

[Pongal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pongal_\(festival\)) is a major and multi-day [harvest festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvest_festival) celebrated by [Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamils).[^cite_note-CushRobinson2008p610-339] It is observed in the month of Thai according to the [Tamil solar calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_calendar) and usually falls on 14 or 15 January.[^cite_note-Beteille73-340] It is dedicated to the [Surya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya), the Sun God and the festival is named after the ceremonial "Pongal", which means "to boil, overflow" and refers to the traditional dish prepared from the new harvest of rice boiled in milk with [jaggery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaggery) offered to Surya.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] Mattu Pongal is meant for celebration of [cattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cattle) when the cattle are bathed, their horns polished and painted in bright colours, garlands of flowers placed around their necks and processions.[^cite_note-344] [Jallikattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jallikattu) is a traditional event held during the period attracting huge crowds in which a bull is released into a crowd of people, and multiple human participants attempt to grab the large hump on the bull's back with both arms and hang on to it while the bull attempts to escape.[^cite_note-345]



[Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamils) decorate their homes with colourful geometric designs called [kolam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolam) made from rice powder

[^cite_note-mercer22-346]

[Puthandu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puthandu) is known as Tamil New Year which marks the first day of year on the [Tamil calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_calendar). The festival date is set with the solar cycle of the solar [Hindu calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_calendar), as the first day of the Tamil month *Chithirai* and falls on or about 14 April every year on the [Gregorian calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorian_calendar).[^cite_note-347] [Karthikai Deepam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karthikai_Deepam) is a festival of lights that is observed on the [full moon day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amavasya) of the [Kartika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C4%81rtika_\(month\)) month, called the [Kartika Pournami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kartik_Purnima), falling on the Gregorian months of November or December.[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349] [Thaipusam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thaipusam) is a Tamil festival celebrated on the first full moon day of the Tamil month of Thai coinciding with [Pusam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pushya) star and dedicated to lord [Murugan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murugan). [Kavadi Aattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavadi_Aattam) is a ceremonial act of sacrifice and offering practiced by devotees which is a central part of Thaipusam and emphasises debt bondage.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] [Aadi Perukku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aadi_Perukku) is a Tamil cultural festival celebrated on the 18th day of the Tamil month of [Adi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aadi_\(month\)) which pays tribute to [water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water)'s life-sustaining properties. The worship of [Amman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariamman) and [Ayyanar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayyanar) deities are organised during the month in temples across Tamil Nadu with much fanfare.[^cite_note-AA-324] [Panguni Uthiram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panguni_Uthiram) is marked on the [purnima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purnima) (full moon) of the month of [Panguni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phalguna) and celebrates the wedding of various Hindu gods.[^cite_note-352]

[Tyagaraja Aradhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyagaraja_Aradhana) is an annual music festival devoted to composer [Tyagaraja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyagaraja). In [Tiruvaiyaru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruvaiyaru) in [Thanjavur district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur_district), thousands of music artists congregate every year.[^cite_note-353] [Chennaiyil Thiruvaiyaru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennaiyil_Thiruvaiyaru) is a music festival which has been conducted from 18 to 25 December every year in Chennai.[^cite_note-354] [Chennai Sangamam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Sangamam) is a large annual open [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_people) cultural festival held in Chennai with the intention of rejuvenating the old village festivals, art and artists.[^cite_note-355] [Madras Music Season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Music_Season), initiated by [Madras Music Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Music_Academy) in 1927, is celebrated every year during the month of December and features performances of traditional [Carnatic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_music) by artists from the city.[^cite_note-356]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Tamil_Nadu)

As of December 2023, Tamil Nadu's [GSDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_state_product) was [₹](490783.md)27.22 trillion (US\$280 billion), [second](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP) highest amongst Indian states, which had grown significantly from [₹](490783.md)2.19 trillion (US\$23 billion) in 2004.[^cite_note-GSDP-357] The per-capita [NDSP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP_per_capita) is [₹](490783.md)315,220 (US\$3,300).[^cite_note-NSDP-358] Tamil Nadu is the most urbanised state in India.[^cite_note-359] Though the state had the lowest percentage of people under the poverty line, rural unemployment rate is considerably higher at 47 per thousand compared to the national average of 28.[^cite_note-PL-212][^cite_note-360] As of 2020, the state had the most number of factories at 38,837 units with an engaged work-force of 2.6 million.[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362]

The state has a diversified industrial base anchored by different sectors including [automobiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile), [software services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_services), [hardware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_hardware), [textiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textiles), [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare) and [financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_services).[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] As of 2022, services contributed to 55% of the GSDP followed by manufacturing at 32% and agriculture at 13%.[^cite_note-TNB-365] There are 42 [Special Economic Zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Economic_Zone) (SEZ) in the state.[^cite_note-366] As per a report by Government of India, Tamil Nadu is the most export competitive state of India in 2023.[^cite_note-367]

### Services



[Tidel Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidel_Park), the first [IT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IT) [Special Economic Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Economic_Zone) in the state

As of 2022, the state is amongst the major [Information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) (IT) exporters of India with a value of ₹576.87 billion (US\$6.0 billion).[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] Established in 2000, [Tidel Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidel_Park) in Chennai was amongst the first and largest IT parks in Asia.[^cite_note-370] The presence of [SEZs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEZ) and government policies have contributed to the growth of the sector which has attracted foreign investments and job seekers from other parts of the country.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] In the 2020s, Chennai has become a major provider of [SaaS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SaaS) and has been dubbed the "SaaS Capital of India".[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374]

The state has two [stock exchanges](28168.md), [Coimbatore Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_Stock_Exchange), established in 2013, and [Madras Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Stock_Exchange), established in 2015 and India's third-largest by trading volume.[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] The [Madras Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Madras_Bank_\(1683\)), the first European-style banking system in India, was established on 21 June 1683, followed by the first commercial banks such as Bank of Hindustan (1770) and General Bank of India (1786).[^cite_note-377] The [Bank of Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Madras) merged with two other presidency banks to form the [Imperial Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Bank_of_India) in 1921 which in 1955 became the [State Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Bank_of_India), the largest bank in India.[^cite_note-378] More than 400 financial industry businesses including three [banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_India) are headquartered in the state.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] The state hosts the [south zonal office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India,_South_Zonal_Office) of the [Reserve Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India), the country's central bank, along with its zonal training centre and staff college at Chennai.[^cite_note-382] There is a [permanent back office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank_office,_Chennai) of the [World Bank](36752.md) in the state.[^cite_note-383]

### Manufacturing



[Arjun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arjun_\(tank\)) battle tanks are manufactured by [AVANI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_Vehicles_Nigam_Limited) in Chennai

Manufacturing in various sectors is governed by the state owned industrial corporation [Tamil Nadu Industrial Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Industrial_Development_Corporation) (TIDCO) apart from central government owned companies. Electronics hardware is a major manufacturing industry with an output of \$5.37 billion in 2023, largest amongst Indian states.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] A large number of automotive companies have their manufacturing bases in the state with the [automotive industry in Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Chennai) accounting for more than 35% of India's overall automotive components and automobile output, earning the nickname "[Detroit of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_of_India)".[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] The [Integral Coach Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integral_Coach_Factory) in Chennai manufactures railway coaches and other rolling stock for [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways).[^cite_note-389]

Another major industry is textiles with the state being home to more than half of the operating fiber textile mills in India.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391] Coimbatore is often referred to as the *[Manchester](20206.md) of [South India](678583.md)* due to its cotton production and textile industries.[^cite_note-392] As of 2022, Tiruppur exported garments worth US\$480 billion, contributing to nearly 54% of the all the textile exports from India and the city is known as the *knitwear capital* due to its cotton knitwear export.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394] As of 2015, the textile industry in Tamil Nadu accounts for 17% of the total invested capital in all the industries.[^cite_note-395] As of 2021, 40% of leather goods exported from India worth ₹92.52 billion (US\$960 million) are being manufactured in the state.[^cite_note-396] The state supplies two-thirds of India's requirements of motors and pumps, and is one of the largest exporters of [wet grinders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet_grinder) with "[Coimbatore Wet Grinder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_Wet_Grinder)", a recognised [Geographical indication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_indication).[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398]

There are two ordnance factories in Aruvankadu and [Tiruchirappalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordnance_Factory_Tiruchirappalli).[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] [AVANI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_Vehicles_Nigam_Limited), headquartered in Chennai, manufactures [armoured fighting vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_fighting_vehicle), [main battle tanks](26595097.md), tank engines and armored clothing for the use of the [Indian Armed Forces](7171338.md).[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] [ISRO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISRO), the Indian space agency, operates a propulsion facility at [Mahendragiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISRO_Propulsion_Complex).[^cite_note-403]

### Agriculture



[Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple food grain and the state is one of the largest producers in India

Agriculture contributes 13% to the GSDP and is a major employment generator in rural areas.[^cite_note-TNB-365] As of 2022, the state had 6.34 million hectares under cultivation.[^cite_note-Agri-404][^cite_note-405] [Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple food grain with the state being one of the largest producers in India with an output of 7.9 million tonnes in 2021–22.[^cite_note-406] The Kaveri delta region is known as the *Rice Bowl of Tamil Nadu*.[^cite_note-407] Among non-food grains, [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane) is the major crop with an annual output of 16.1 million tonnes in 2021–22.[^cite_note-408] The state is a producer of [spices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spices) and is the top producer of [oil seeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_seed), [tapioca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapioca), [cloves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clove) and [flowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flower) in India.[^cite_note-409] The state accounts for 6.5% of fruit and 4.2% of vegetables production in the country.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411] The state is a leading producer of [banana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana) and [mango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mango) with more than 78% of the area under fruit cultivation.[^cite_note-412] As of 2019, the state was the second largest producer in India of [natural rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_rubber) and [coconuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut).[^cite_note-413] [Tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea) is a popular crop in hill-stations with the state being a major producer of a unique flavoured [Nilgiri tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_tea).[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415]

As of 2022, the state is the largest producer in India of [poultry](23197.md) and [eggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eggs_as_food) with an annual production of 20.8 billion units, contributing to more than 16% of the national output.[^cite_note-416] The state has a fishermen population of 1.05 million and the coast consists of 3 major fishing harbors, 3 medium fishing harbors and 363 fish landing centres.[^cite_note-417] As of 2022, the fishing output was 0.8 million tonnes with a contribution of 5% to the total fish production in India.[^cite_note-418] Aquaculture includes [shrimp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrimp), [sea weed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_weed), [mussel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mussel), [clam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clam) and [oyster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oyster) farming across more than 6000 hectares.[^cite_note-419] [M. S. Swaminathan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._S._Swaminathan), known as the "father of the Indian [Green Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Revolution_in_India)" was from Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-420]

## Infrastructure

### Water supply



[Kaveri](177299.md) river is one of the major water sources in the state

Tamil Nadu accounts for nearly 4% of the land area and 6% of the population of India, but has only 3% of the water resources of the country. The per capita water availability is 800 m<sup>3</sup> (28,000 cu ft) which is lower than the national average of 2,300 m<sup>3</sup> (81,000 cu ft).[^cite_note-WR-421] The state is dependent on the monsoons for replenishing the water resources. There are 17 major river basins with 61 reservoirs and about 41,948 tanks with a total surface water potential of 24,864 million cubic metres (MCM), 90% of which is used for [irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irrigation). The utilisable groundwater recharge is 22,423 MCM.[^cite_note-WR-421] The major rivers include [Kaveri](177299.md), [Bhavani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhavani_River), [Vaigai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaigai) and [Thamirabarani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thamirabarani). With most of the rivers originating from other states, Tamil Nadu depends on neighbouring states for considerable quantum of water which has often led to [disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaveri_River_water_dispute).[^cite_note-422] The state has 116 [large dams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_dams_and_reservoirs_in_Tamil_Nadu).[^cite_note-423] Apart from the rivers, the majority of the water comes from rainwater stored in more than 41,000 [tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanks) and 1.68 million [wells](15105978.md) across the state.[^cite_note-Agri-404]

Water supply and sewage treatment are managed by the respective local administrative bodies such as the [Chennai MetroWater Supply and Sewage Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_MetroWater_Supply_and_Sewage_Board) in Chennai.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] [Desalination](156787.md) plants including the country's largest at [Minjur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minjur) provide alternative means of drinking water.[^cite_note-426] As per the 2011 census, only 83.4% of the households have access to safe drinking water, less than the national average of 85.5%.[^cite_note-427] Water sources are also threatened by [environmental pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_pollution) and effluent discharge from industries.[^cite_note-428]

### Health and sanitation



The [General Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_General_Hospital,_Chennai) in Chennai was established on 16 November 1664 and was the first major hospital in India

The state is one of the [leading states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_states_ranking_by_prevalence_of_open_defecation) in terms of sanitation facilities with more than 99.96% of people having access to toilets.[^cite_note-429] The state has robust health facilities and ranks higher in all health related parameters such as high life expectancy of 74 years (sixth) and 98.4% [institutional delivery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_states_ranking_by_institutional_delivery) (second).[^cite_note-LE-211][^cite_note-430] Of the three demographically related targets of the [Millennium Development Goals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Development_Goals) set by the [United Nations](31769.md) and expected to be achieved by 2015, Tamil Nadu achieved the goals related to improvement of maternal health and of reducing infant mortality and child mortality by 2009.[^cite_note-IMR-431][^cite_note-432]

The health infrastructure in the state includes both government-run and private hospitals. As of 2023, the state had 404 public hospitals, 1,776 public dispensaries, 11,030 health centres and 481 mobile units run by the government with a capacity of more than 94,700 beds.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434] The [General Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_General_Hospital,_Chennai) in Chennai was established on 16 November 1664 and was the first major hospital in India.[^cite_note-435] The state government administers free [polio vaccine](192198.md) for eligible age groups.[^cite_note-436] Tamil Nadu is a major centre for medical tourism and Chennai is termed as "India's health capital".[^cite_note-437] [Medical tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_tourism) forms an important part of the economy with more than 40% of total medical tourists visiting India making it to Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-438]

### Communication

Tamil Nadu is one of four Indian states connected by [undersea fibre-optic cables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_communications_cable).[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] As of 2023, four mobile phone service companies operate [GSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSM) networks including [Bharti Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel), [BSNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BSNL), [Vodafone Idea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea) and [Reliance Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reliance_Jio) offering [4G](486547.md) and [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) mobile services.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-TRAI-443] Wireline and broadband services are offered by five major operators and other smaller local operators.[^cite_note-TRAI-443] Tamil Nadu is amongst the states with a high internet usage and penetration.[^cite_note-444] In 2018, the state government unveiled a plan to lay 55,000 km (34,000 mi) of [optical fiber](3372377.md) across the state to provide high-speed internet.[^cite_note-445]

### Power and energy



[Kudankulam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kudankulam_Nuclear_Power_Plant), the largest nuclear power station in India

Electricity distribution in the state is done by the [Tamil Nadu Electricity Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Electricity_Board) headquartered at Chennai.[^cite_note-446] As of 2023, the average daily consumption is 15,000 MW. Only 40% of the power is generated locally, with the remaining 60% met through purchases.[^cite_note-447] As of 2022, the state was the fourth largest power consumer with a per capita availability of 1588.7 Kwh.[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] As of 2023, the state has the [third highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_of_India_by_installed_power_capacity) installed power capacity of 38,248 MW with 54.6% from renewable resources.[^cite_note-PW-450][^cite_note-451] [Thermal power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_power) is the largest contributor with more than 10,000 MW.[^cite_note-PW-450] Tamil Nadu is the only state with two operational [nuclear power plants](207347.md). The plant at [Kalpakkam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Atomic_Power_Station) is the first fully indigenous nuclear power station in India. The [Kudankulam Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kudankulam_Nuclear_Power_Plant) is the largest nuclear power station in India. It generates nearly one-third of the total nuclear power generated in the country.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] Tamil Nadu has the largest established wind power capacity in India with over 8,000 MW, mostly based out of two regions, [Palghat Gap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palghat_Gap) and [Muppandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muppandal_Wind_Farm). The latter is one of the [largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_power_stations#Wind) operational onshore wind farms in the world.[^cite_note-455]

### Media



Headquarters of [Sun Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Network), India's largest private TV broadcaster

Newspaper publishing started in the state started with the launch of the weekly *[The Madras Courier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Madras_Courier)* in 1785.[^cite_note-456] It was followed by the weeklies *[Madras Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Gazette)* and *[Government Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Gazette)* in 1795.[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458] *[The Spectator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Spectator_\(Indian_newspaper\))*, founded in 1836 was the first English newspaper to be owned by an Indian and became the first daily newspaper in 1853.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] The first Tamil newspaper, *[Swadesamitran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swadesamitran)* was launched in 1899.[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462] The state has a number of newspapers and magazines published in various languages including Tamil, English and Telugu.[^cite_note-463] The major dailies with more than 100,000 circulation per day include *[The Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hindu)*, *[Dina Thanthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dina_Thanthi)*, *[Dinakaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinakaran)*, *[The Times of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India)*, *[Dina Malar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dina_Malar)*, and *[The Deccan Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Deccan_Chronicle)*.[^cite_note-464] Several periodicals and local newspapers prevalent in select localities also bring out editions from multiple cities.[^cite_note-465]

Government-run [Doordarshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doordarshan) broadcasts terrestrial and [satellite television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_television) channels from its Chennai centre set up in 1974.[^cite_note-466] [DD Podhigai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DD_Podhigai), Doordarshan's Tamil language regional channel was launched on 14 April 1993.[^cite_note-467] There are more than 30 private satellite television networks including [Sun Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Network), one of India's largest broadcasting companies is the state, established in 1993.[^cite_note-468] The cable TV service is entirely controlled by the state government while [DTH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_broadcast_satellite) and [IPTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPTV) is available via various private operators.[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470] [Radio broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_broadcasting) began in 1924 by the Madras Presidency Radio Club.[^cite_note-471] [All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio) was established in 1938.[^cite_note-472] There are many [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amplitude_modulation) and [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frequency_modulation) radio stations operated by All India Radio, [Hello FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hello_FM), [Suryan FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suryan_FM), [Radio Mirchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Mirchi), [Radio City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_City_\(Indian_radio_station\)) and [BIG FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BIG_FM_92.7) among others.[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474] In 2006, the government of Tamil Nadu distributed free televisions to all families, which has led to high penetration of television services.[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476] From the early 2010s, [Direct to Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_to_Home) has become increasingly popular replacing cable television services.[^cite_note-477] [Tamil television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Tamil-language_television_channels) serials form a major prime time source of entertainment.[^cite_note-478]

### Others

Fire services are handled by the [Tamil Nadu Fire and Rescue Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Fire_and_Rescue_Services) which operates 356 operating fire stations.[^cite_note-479] Postal service is handled by [India Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_Post), which operates more than 11,800 post offices in the state.[^cite_note-480] The first post office was established at Fort St. George on 1 June 1786.[^cite_note-481]

## Transportation

### Roads



Highways network of Tamil Nadu

Main article: [Road network in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_network_in_Tamil_Nadu)

Tamil Nadu has an extensive road network covering about 271,000 km as of 2023 with a road density of 2,084.71 kilometres (1,295.38 mi) per 1000 km<sup>2</sup> which is higher than the national average of 1,926.02 kilometres (1,196.77 mi) per 1000 km<sup>2</sup>.[^cite_note-Policy-482] The [Highways Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Highways_and_Minor_Ports_\(Tamil_Nadu\)) (HD) of the state was established in April 1946 and is responsible for construction and maintenance of [national highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Highways_in_India#Tamil_Nadu), state highways, major district roads and other roads in the state.[^cite_note-TNGov-483] It operates through eleven wings with 120 divisions and maintains 73,187 kilometres (45,476 mi) of highways in the state.[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-HW-485]

| Type | NH | SH | MDR | ODR | OR | Total |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Length (km) | 6,805 | 12,291 | 12,034 | 42,057 | 197,542 | **271,000** |



[Kathipara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathipara_Junction) [interchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloverleaf_interchange) in Chennai

There are 48 National Highways in Tamil Nadu, totalling 6,805 kilometres (4,228 mi) in length. The National Highways Wing of the state highways department, established in 1971, is responsible for the maintenance of National Highways, as laid down by [National Highways Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highways_Authority_of_India) (NHAI).[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487] There are also [state highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_state_highways_in_Tamil_Nadu), totalling 6,805 kilometres (4,228 mi) long, which connect [district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District) headquarters, important towns and national highways in the state.[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-HW-485] As of 2020, 32,598 buses are operated with the state transport units operating 20,946 buses along with 7,596 private buses and 4,056 mini buses.[^cite_note-TNSTC-489] [Tamil Nadu State Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_State_Transport_Corporation) (TNSTC), established in 1947 when private buses operating in [Madras presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_presidency) were nationalised, is the primary [public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport) bus operator in the state.[^cite_note-TNSTC-489] It operates buses along intra and inter state bus routes, as well as city routes with eight divisions including the [State Express Transport Corporation Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Express_Transport_Corporation_\(Tamil_Nadu\)) (SETC) which runs long-distance express services. Metropolitan Transport Corporation in Chennai and State Express Transport Corporation.[^cite_note-TNSTC-489][^cite_note-490] As of 2020, Tamil Nadu had 32.1 million registered vehicles.[^cite_note-RV-491]

### Rail



[Chennai Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Central) is a major rail station



[Chennai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Metro), the only operational metro rail in the state

Further information: [Southern Railway zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Railway_zone)

The rail network in Tamil Nadu forms a part of [Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Railway_zone) of [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways), which is headquartered in Chennai with four divisions in the state namely [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_railway_division), [Tiruchirappalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruchirappalli_railway_division), [Madurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurai_railway_division) and [Salem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_railway_division).[^cite_note-492] As of 2023, the state had a total railway track length of 5,601 km (3,480 mi) covering a route length of 3,858 km (2,397 mi).[^cite_note-SR1-493] There are 532 railway stations in the state with [Chennai Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Central), [Chennai Egmore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Egmore), [Coimbatore Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_Junction) and [Madurai Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurai_Junction) being the top revenue earning stations.[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495] Indian railways also has a coach manufacturing unit at [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integral_Coach_Factory), [electric locomotive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_locomotive) sheds at [Arakkonam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_Loco_Shed,_Arakkonam), [Erode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_Loco_Shed,_Erode) and [Royapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_Loco_Shed,_Royapuram), [diesel locomotive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_locomotive) sheds at [Erode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_Loco_Shed,_Erode), [Tiruchirappalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_Loco_Shed,_Golden_Rock) and [Tondiarpet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_Loco_Shed,_Tondiarpet), [Steam locomotive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steam_locomotive) shed at [Coonoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Mountain_Railway) along with various maintenance depots. [^cite_note-496]

| Route length (km) | Track length (km) |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Electrified | Non electrified | Total |  |  |  |  |  |
