Chennai has a well-established suburban railway network operated by Southern railway, covering 212 km (132 mi) which was established in 1928.[^cite_note-CSR-497][^cite_note-498] The [Mass Rapid Transit System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_Rapid_Transit_System_\(Chennai\)) (MRTS) is an elevated urban mass transit system established in 1995 operating on a single line from [Chennai Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Beach) to [Velachery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velachery_railway_station).[^cite_note-CSR-497][^cite_note-MRTS-499] [Chennai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Metro) is a [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) rail system in Chennai which was opened in 2015 and consists of two operational lines operating across 54.1 km (33.6 mi) in 2023.[^cite_note-500] [Nilgiri Mountain Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Mountain_Railway) is a 1,000 mm (3 ft 3+3⁄8 in) [metre gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre_gauge) railway in [Nilgiris district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiris_district) which was built in 1908 and is the only [rack railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rack_railway) in India.[^cite_note-NMR-501][^cite_note-UNESCO-502][^cite_note-503]

### Air



[Chennai International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_International_Airport) is one of the busiest airports in [South Asia](21566765.md)

Further information: [List of airports in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Tamil_Nadu)

The aviation history of the state began in 1910, when [Giacomo D'Angelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_D'Angelis) built the first powered flight in Asia and tested it in [Island Grounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_Grounds).[^cite_note-504] In 1915, [Tata Air Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Sons) started an airmail service between Karachi and Madras, marking the beginning of civil aviation in India.[^cite_note-505] On 15 October 1932, [J. R. D. Tata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._R._D._Tata) flew a [Puss Moth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Havilland_Puss_Moth) aircraft carrying air mail from Karachi to [Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay)'s [Juhu Airstrip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juhu_aerodrome) and the flight was continued to Madras piloted by aviator [Nevill Vintcent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nevill_Vintcent) marking the first scheduled commercial flight.[^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507] There are three [international](1519705.md), one [limited international](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs_airport) and six domestic or private airports in Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509]



[Chennai port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_port) is amongst India's busiest

[Chennai airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_airport), which is the fourth busiest airport by passenger traffic in India, is a major international airport and the main gateway to the state.[^cite_note-AAI-510] Other international airports in the state include [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_International_Airport) and [Tiruchirapalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiruchirapalli_International_Airport) while [Madurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurai_Airport) is a customs airport with limited international flights.[^cite_note-AAI-510] Domestic flights are operational to certain airports like [Tuticorin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuticorin_Airport) and [Salem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_Airport_\(India\)) while flights are planned to be introduced to more domestic airports by the [UDAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UDAN) scheme of [Government of India](553883.md).[^cite_note-511] The region comes under the purview of the [Southern Air Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Air_Command,_Indian_Air_Force) of the [Indian Air Force](403355.md). The Air Force operates three air bases in the state [Sulur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulur_Air_Force_Station), [Tambaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tambaram_Air_Force_Station) and [Thanjavur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur_Air_Force_Station).[^cite_note-512] The [Indian Navy](604522.md) operates airbases at [Arakkonam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Rajali), [Uchipuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Parundu) and Chennai.[^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514] In 2019, [Indian Space Research Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Space_Research_Organization) (ISRO) announced the setting up a new rocket launch pad near [Kulasekharapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kulasekharapatnam) in [Thoothukudi district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thoothukudi_district).[^cite_note-515]

### Water

There are three major ports, [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Port), [Ennore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamarajar_Port_Limited) and [Thoothukudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V.O._Chidambaranar_Port_Authority), which are managed by the [Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways](17387745.md) of the Government of India.[^cite_note-516] There is an intermediate sea port at [Nagapattinam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagapattinam_Port) and sixteen other minor ports, which are managed by the department of highways and minor ports of the Government of Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-Policy-482] Tamil Nadu forms part of both the [Eastern Naval Command](30139022.md) and [Southern Naval Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Naval_Command) of the Indian Navy, which has a major base at Chennai and logistics support base at [Thoothukudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thoothukudi).[^cite_note-517][^cite_note-518]

## Education

Main article: [Education in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Tamil_Nadu)

Tamil Nadu is one of the most literate states in India, with a literacy rate estimated to be 82.9% as per the 2017 National Statistical Commission survey, higher than the national average of 77.7%.[^cite_note-NSC-206][^cite_note-519] The state had seen one of the highest literacy growth since the 1960s due to the [midday meal scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midday_meal_scheme) introduced on a large scale by K. Kamaraj to increase school enrolment.[^cite_note-520][^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522] The scheme was further upgraded in 1982 to "Nutritious noon-meal scheme" to combat [malnutrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malnutrition_in_India).[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524] As of 2022, the state has one of the highest enrolment to secondary education at 95.6%, far above the national average of 79.6%.[^cite_note-525] An analysis of primary school education by [Pratham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratham) showed a low drop-off rate but poor quality of education compared to certain other states.[^cite_note-526]

As of 2022, the state had over 37,211 government schools, 8,403 government-aided schools and 12,631 private schools which educate 5.47 million, 2.84 million, and 5.69 million students respectively.[^cite_note-527][^cite_note-528] There are 3,12,683 teachers with 80,217 teachers in government-aided schools with an average teacher-pupil ratio of 1\:26.6.[^cite_note-529] Public schools are all affiliated with the [Tamil Nadu State Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Board_of_Secondary_Education), while private schools may be affiliated with either of Tamil Nadu Board of Secondary Education, [Central Board of Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Board_of_Secondary_Education) (CBSE), [Council for the Indian School Certificate Examinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_for_the_Indian_School_Certificate_Examinations) (ICSE) or [National Institute of Open Schooling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Open_Schooling) (NIOS).[^cite_note-530] School education starts with two years of kindergarten from age three onwards and then follows the Indian [10+2 plan](293121.md), ten years of school and two years of higher secondary education.[^cite_note-531]



[University of Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Madras) is one of the oldest universities in India

As of 2023, there are 56 universities in the state including 24 [public universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_universities), four [private universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_universities) and 28 [deemed-to-be universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deemed_university).[^cite_note-532] The [University of Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Madras) was founded in 1857 and is one of India's first modern universities.[^cite_note-533] There are 510 engineering colleges including 34 government colleges in the state.[^cite_note-Gov1-534][^cite_note-535] [Indian Institute of Technology Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Technology_Madras) is a premier institute of engineering and [College of Engineering, Guindy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Engineering,_Guindy), [Anna University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_University) founded in 1794 is the oldest engineering college in India.[^cite_note-536] The [Officers Training Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Officers_Training_Academy) of the [Indian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Army) is headquartered at Chennai.[^cite_note-537] There are also 496 [polytechnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_technology) institutions with 92 government colleges and 935 arts and science colleges in the state including 302 government run colleges.[^cite_note-Gov1-534][^cite_note-538][^cite_note-539] [Madras Christian College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Christian_College) (1837), [Presidency College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_College,_Madras) (1840) and [Pachaiyappa's College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pachaiyappa's_College) (1842) are amongst the oldest arts and science colleges in the country.[^cite_note-540]



[Connemara Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connemara_Public_Library) is one of the oldest and is amongst the four [National Depository Centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Depository_Centre) in India

There are over 870 medical, nursing and dental colleges in the state including 21 for traditional medicine and four for modern medicine.[^cite_note-541] The [Madras Medical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Medical_College) was established in 1835 and is one of the oldest medical colleges in India.[^cite_note-542] As per the [National Institutional Ranking Framework](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institutional_Ranking_Framework) (NIRF) rankings in 2023, 26 universities, 15 engineering, 35 arts science, 8 management and 8 medical colleges from the state are ranked amongst the top 100 in the country.[^cite_note-543][^cite_note-544] As of 2023, the state has a 69% reservation in educational institutions for socially [backward sections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_denotified_communities_of_Tamil_Nadu) of society, the highest among all Indian states.[^cite_note-545] There are ten [institutes of national importance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutes_of_National_Importance) in the state.[^cite_note-546] Research institutes including [Tamil Nadu Agricultural University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Agricultural_University), [Central Institute for Cotton Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Institute_for_Cotton_Research), [Sugarcane Breeding Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane_Breeding_Research_Institute), [Institute of Forest Genetics and Tree Breeding (IFGTB)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Forest_Genetics_and_Tree_Breeding_\(IFGTB\)) and [Indian Council of Forestry Research and Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Council_of_Forestry_Research_and_Education) are involved in agricultural research.[^cite_note-547][^cite_note-548][^cite_note-549]

As of 2023, the state has 4,622 public libraries.[^cite_note-550] Established in 1896, [Connemara Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connemara_Public_Library) is one of the oldest and is amongst the four [National Depository Centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Depository_Centre) in India that receive a copy of all newspapers and books published in the country. The [Anna Centenary Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Centenary_Library) is the largest library in Asia.[^cite_note-551][^cite_note-552] There are many research institutions spread across the state.[^cite_note-553] Chennai book fair is an annual book fair organised by the Booksellers and Publishers Association of South India (BAPASI) and is typically held in December–January.[^cite_note-554]

## Tourism and recreation

Main article: [Tourism in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Tamil_Nadu)



[Nilgiri Mountain Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Mountain_Railway) in the [Nilgiri hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_hills) is a declared [World Heritage Site](44940.md)

With its diverse culture and architecture and varied geographies, Tamil Nadu has a robust tourism industry. In 1971, the [Government of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Tamil_Nadu) established the [Tamil Nadu Tourism Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Tourism_Development_Corporation), which is the nodal agency responsible for the promotion of [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism) and development of tourist related infrastructure in the state.[^cite_note-555] It is managed by the [Tourism, Culture and Religious Endowments Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Religious_and_Charitable_Endowments_Department).[^cite_note-556] The tag line "Enchanting Tamil Nadu" was adopted in the tourism promotions.[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-EC-558] In the 21st century, the state has been amongst the top destinations for domestic and international tourists.[^cite_note-EC-558][^cite_note-559] As of 2020, Tamil Nadu recorded the most tourist foot-falls with more than 140.7 million tourists visiting the state.[^cite_note-CR-560]

Tamil Nadu's coastline is 1,076 kilometres (669 mi) long, with many beaches dotting the coast.[^cite_note-561] [Marina Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_Beach) spanning 13 km (8.1 mi) is the second-longest urban beach in the world.[^cite_note-562] As the state is straddled by the Western and Eastern ghats, it is home to many hill stations, popular amongst them are [Udagamandalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udagamandalam) (Ooty) situated in the [Nilgiri Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Hills) and [Kodaikanal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodaikanal) in the Palani hills.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] There are a number of [rock-cut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_rock-cut_architecture) cave-temples and more than 34,000 temples in Tamil Nadu built across various periods some of which are several centuries old.[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566] With many rivers and streams, there are a number of waterfalls in the state including the [Courtallam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courtallam) and [Hogenakkal Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hogenakkal_Falls).[^cite_note-567][^cite_note-568] There are four [World Heritage Sites](44940.md) declared by [UNESCO](21786641.md) in the state: [Group of Monuments at Mahabalipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_Monuments_at_Mahabalipuram),[^cite_note-569] [Great Living Chola Temples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Living_Chola_Temples),[^cite_note-Great_Living_Chola_Temples-44] [Nilgiri Mountain Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Mountain_Railway),[^cite_note-NMR-501][^cite_note-UNESCO-502] and [Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilgiri_Biosphere_Reserve).[^cite_note-570][^cite_note-571]

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Tamil_Nadu)



[Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi) is the state game of Tamil Nadu

[Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi) is a [contact sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contact_sport) which is the state game of Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573] [Pro Kabaddi League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Kabaddi_League) is the most popular region based franchise tournament with [Tamil Thalaivas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Thalaivas) representing the state.[^cite_note-574][^cite_note-575] [Chess](5134.md) is a popular [board game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_game) which originated as [Sathurangam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaturanga) in the 7th century CE.[^cite_note-576] Chennai is often dubbed "India's chess capital" as the city is home to multiple chess [grandmasters](12304.md), including former world champion [Viswanathan Anand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viswanathan_Anand). The state played host to the [World Chess Championship 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Chess_Championship_2013) and [44th Chess Olympiad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/44th_Chess_Olympiad) in 2022.[^cite_note-577][^cite_note-578][^cite_note-579][^cite_note-580] [Traditional games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_games_of_India) like [Pallanguzhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallanguzhi),[^cite_note-581] [Uriyadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Tamil_Nadu#Uriyadi),[^cite_note-582] [Gillidanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gillidanda),[^cite_note-583] and [Dhaayam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pachisi) are played across the region.[^cite_note-584] [Jallikattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jallikattu) and [Rekla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rekla) are traditional sporting events involving [bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bull).[^cite_note-585][^cite_note-586] Traditional martial arts include [Silambattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silambattam),[^cite_note-587] [Gatta gusthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatta_gusthi),[^cite_note-588] and [Adimurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adimurai).[^cite_note-589]



[M. A. Chidambaram Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._A._Chidambaram_Stadium) in Chennai, one of the oldest cricketing venues

Cricket is the most popular sport in the state.[^cite_note-590] The [M.A. Chidambaram Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M.A._Chidambaram_Stadium) established in 1916 is among the oldest cricket stadiums in India and has hosted matches during multiple [ICC Cricket World Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICC_Cricket_World_Cup).[^cite_note-591][^cite_note-592][^cite_note-593] Established in 1987, [MRF Pace Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRF_Pace_Foundation) is a [bowling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowling_\(cricket\)) academy based in Chennai.[^cite_note-594] Chennai is home to the most successful [Indian Premier League](13260340.md) (IPL) cricket team, [Chennai Super Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Super_Kings), and hosted the finals during the [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_premier_league_2011),[^cite_note-595] [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Indian_Premier_League),[^cite_note-596] and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indian_Premier_League) seasons.[^cite_note-597] [Football](10568.md) is also popular with the [Indian Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Super_League) being the major club competition and [Chennaiyin FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennaiyin_FC) representing the state.[^cite_note-598][^cite_note-599][^cite_note-600]

There are multi-purpose venues in major cities including [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Stadium_\(Chennai\)) and [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Stadium_\(Coimbatore\)), which host [football](10568.md) and [athletics](43719.md). Chennai also houses a multi–purpose indoor complex for [volleyball](32558.md), [basketball](3921.md), [kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi) and [table tennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_tennis).[^cite_note-601][^cite_note-SDAT-602] Chennai hosted the [1995 South Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_South_Asian_Games).[^cite_note-603] Tamil Nadu Hockey Association is the governing body of [hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_Hockey) in the state and [Mayor Radhakrishnan Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_Radhakrishnan_Stadium) in Chennai was the venue for the international hockey tournaments, the [2005 Men's Champions Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Men's_Hockey_Champions_Trophy) and the [2007 Men's Asia Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Men's_Hockey_Asia_Cup).[^cite_note-604] [Madras Boat Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Boat_Club) (founded in 1846) and [Royal Madras Yacht Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Madras_Yacht_Club) (founded in 1911) promote [sailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sailing), [rowing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowing) and [canoeing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canoeing) sports in Chennai.[^cite_note-605] Inaugurated in 1990, [Madras Motor Race Track](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Motor_Race_Track) was the first permanent racing circuit in India and hosts formula racing events.[^cite_note-606] Coimbatore is often referred to as "India's Motorsports Hub" and the "Backyard of Indian Motorsports" and hosts the [Kari Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kari_Motor_Speedway), a Formula 3 Category circuit.[^cite_note-607][^cite_note-608] [Horse racing](74711.md) is held at the [Guindy Race Course](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guindy_Race_Course) and the state has three 18-hole golf courses, the [Cosmopolitan Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmopolitan_Club_\(Chennai\)), the [Gymkhana Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymkhana_Club,_Chennai) and the [Coimbatore Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore_Golf_Club).[^cite_note-609]
